Um... I guess it depends on what you mean by a large amount? I mean, I don't think it's anything too crazy, but... well, sometimes if I've had a really long day, especially after a late shift at the shelter, I might eat a bit more than usual.

I mean, it is definitely more than I would ever eat if someone else was there with me. But I'm just so hungry after running around with the dogs all day. I hope that doesn't sound too terrible? I'm just trying to be completely honest with you so you have what you need.

Um... well, I guess... I mean, maybe a little bit? But I really don't think it's, like, a crazy amount or anything.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, I don't really think it's an emotional thing? I mean, I wouldn't say I feel sad or anxious or anything like that. Honestly, I really like my job, and working with the animals keeps me pretty upbeat. 

I think it's really just that I'm physically wiped out. By the time I get home, my body is just telling me it needs energy after running around the kennels all day. So I don't think I'm eating because of my feelings or because I'm bored. It's just physical hunger, I think. 

That's pretty standard, right? Like, eating just because you're tired and need to recharge, not because you're upset? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking... I hope that's helpful.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm totally out of control or anything... 

But, um, I guess that is sort of why I decided to come in. It's just that sometimes, when I'm eating alone at night, it feels a little bit like... like maybe something just kind of takes over for a minute? And I find it a bit hard to stop, even when I'm sitting there thinking to myself, "Okay, I should probably put this away now." 

It's not like I'm completely losing my mind or anything! I just get sort of... stuck in the motion of eating, I think? I'm sure a lot of people get carried away with a snack sometimes, right? Like, it's pretty normal to have a hard time putting down a bag of chips when you're tired? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it really is. I hope that makes sense?

Um... well, I guess I do usually eat when I'm alone, yeah. But it's not like I'm hiding it or anything! It's just that I live by myself, and I work late, so there's usually no one else around anyway.

It's quiet, and I finally have a moment to myself, so I just sort of relax with some food. It's not like I'm crying or having a panic attack or anything dramatic like that. I'm usually just feeling a bit tired and, I don't know, maybe a little bored? \n\nI really try to keep a positive attitude about things. I hope that makes sense? I try to give myself some grace, especially since I'm on my feet all day. \n\nIs... is it bad if I don't feel guilty? I hope it's okay that I'm saying that. Um... well, no, I don't think I eat super fast or anything like that. I mean, maybe a little quicker than if I was sitting down for a nice dinner at a restaurant, but... I don't think it's anything crazy?

I'll be standing there in the kitchen, literally thinking to myself, 'Okay, you need to put this away now, just go to bed,' but my hands just keep going. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit that out loud, honestly, because I know I should have more willpower. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day as well, or is it mainly at night?\n\nPatient: Um... mostly it's just at night. I mean, during the day, I'm at work—I work with animals, so I'm pretty busy and distracted. Sometimes if I have a really stressful afternoon, I might, you know, have a little extra snack when I get home, but it's not... it doesn't feel like that same out-of-control feeling. \n\nIt's really just when it gets late and quiet, and I'm by myself. I try to be so good during the day, I really do. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word 'ashamed,' really. That sounds a bit... intense, I guess? \n\nI mean, I definitely feel a little silly about it. Maybe just a bit disappointed in my lack of willpower, like I mentioned. But I try not to dwell on it too much, you know? I just kind of wake up the next morning and tell myself that I'll do better that day, and focus on the animals and my work. \n\nI'm not really sure if that's exactly what you meant by guilty? I really hope I'm answering these right for you. I just don't want to make it sound like some huge, dramatic thing when it's probably just me needing to be a bit more disciplined.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, my weight stays pretty much the same, honestly. I try to keep pretty active with the animals and all, so I guess it balances out? \n\nI might fluctuate a pound or two here and there, just like anyone else, but nothing... nothing significant or anything that anyone would notice. I try to take good care of myself, overall. Is that... is that normal? I hope that's okay to say. I just haven't seen any big changes on the scale or anything.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these late-night eating episodes, you're consuming a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it depends on what you mean by a large amount? I mean, I don't think it's anything too crazy, but... well, sometimes if I've had a really long day, especially after a late shift at the shelter, I might eat a bit more than usual. \n\nLike, I might make a frozen pizza, and then... maybe have some cereal, just straight out of the box because I don't want to make more dishes, you know? And maybe whatever leftovers are in the fridge, or some snacks from the cabinet. It all just kind of happens in, I don't know, an hour or two while I'm winding down. \n\nI mean, it is definitely more than I would ever eat if someone else was there with me. But I'm just so hungry after running around with the dogs all day. I hope that doesn't sound too terrible? I'm just trying to be completely honest with you so you have what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say I'm really stressed or upset, exactly. I mean, sure, the shelter can be a bit chaotic sometimes, but I love the animals, so it's a good kind of busy, you know? \n\nI think it's more just... well, it's just me unwinding after a long day. It's quiet, and I finally have a moment to myself, so I just sort of relax with some food. It's not like I'm crying or having a panic attack or anything dramatic like that. I'm usually just feeling a bit tired and, I don't know, maybe a little bored? \n\nI really try to keep a positive attitude about things. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... I don't think so? Not really guilty, no. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes the next morning I might think, 'Oh, I probably didn't need to eat quite all of that,' mostly just because I feel a little full or sluggish when I'm getting ready for work. But I try not to be too hard on myself about it. It's just food, you know? I'm not, like, beating myself up or sitting there feeling terrible about myself. I try to give myself some grace, especially since I'm on my feet all day. \n\nIs... is it bad if I don't feel guilty? I hope it's okay that I'm saying that. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating more quickly than usual during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think I eat any faster than normal? I mean, I'm usually just sitting on the couch, maybe watching a show or something while I eat. \n\nIt's not like I'm racing against a clock or shoving food in my mouth as fast as I can. I just kind of eat at a normal pace, I guess. Sometimes I might eat a little quicker just because I'm so tired and I want to get to bed, but it's nothing extreme. \n\nI try to just relax and enjoy it, you know? Does that sound normal to you? I hope I'm explaining it right. I just don't want you to think I'm doing anything too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... I mean, maybe a little bit? But I really don't think it's, like, a crazy amount or anything. \n\nI mean, when I get home after a late shift at the shelter, I'm just so hungry, you know? Being on my feet all day with the dogs and cleaning... it takes a lot out of you. So, yeah, sometimes I might have, like, a frozen pizza. And maybe some cereal if I'm still feeling a bit hungry after that. Or just whatever leftovers or snacks are around. It probably happens over, like, an hour or two while I'm winding down. \n\nI guess if you put it all together, it might sound like a lot to some people, and I probably wouldn't eat quite that much if I had company over... but I'm just trying to refuel after a long day. That makes sense, right? Like, it's normal to be really hungry after working hard? I just want to make sure you understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating is usually triggered by a specific emotional state, such as feeling sad, anxious, or bored?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't really think it's an emotional thing? I mean, I wouldn't say I feel sad or anxious or anything like that. Honestly, I really like my job, and working with the animals keeps me pretty upbeat. \n\nI think it's really just that I'm physically wiped out. By the time I get home, my body is just telling me it needs energy after running around the kennels all day. So I don't think I'm eating because of my feelings or because I'm bored. It's just physical hunger, I think. \n\nThat's pretty standard, right? Like, eating just because you're tired and need to recharge, not because you're upset? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking... I hope that's helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm totally out of control or anything... \n\nBut, um, I guess that is sort of why I decided to come in. It's just that sometimes, when I'm eating alone at night, it feels a little bit like... like maybe something just kind of takes over for a minute? It's just that, like I said, I'm so tired, and it just kind of happens. But I'm definitely aware of it, you know? I mean, maybe a little quicker than if I was sitting down for a nice dinner at a restaurant, but... I don't think it's anything crazy? \n\nI just kind of eat at a normal pace, I guess. I mean, I'm usually just watching TV or something while I eat, so I'm not, like, shoveling it in. It's more just... steady, I suppose? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess it might be a little more than a normal dinner, maybe? I don't know if I'd say it's, like, a huge amount or anything crazy. 

I mean, on nights when I've had a really late shift at the shelter, I get so hungry. Sometimes I'll make a frozen pizza, and... well, I might eat the whole thing. And then maybe have some cereal, just, you know, out of the box while I'm watching TV. And if there are leftovers or snacks in the cabinet, I might have some of those too, over the course of an hour or two. 

I know it sounds like a lot when I list it out like that, but I'm just so famished after work! I definitely wouldn't eat that much if I was out with friends or something. I just don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or anything like that. It's just a hungry guy unwinding, right? I hope that makes sense and doesn't sound too bad?

It probably happens over, like, an hour or two while I'm winding down. \n\nI guess if you put it all together, it might sound like a lot to some people, and I probably wouldn't eat quite that much if I had company over... but I'm just trying to refuel after a long day. That makes sense, right? Like, it's normal to be really hungry after working hard? Honestly, I really like my job, and working with the animals keeps me pretty upbeat. \n\nI think it's really just that I'm physically wiped out. By the time I get home, my body is just telling me it needs energy after running around the kennels all day. So I don't think I'm eating because of my feelings or because I'm bored. It's just physical hunger, I think. \n\nThat's pretty standard, right? Like, eating just because you're tired and need to recharge, not because you're upset? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking... I hope that's helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm totally out of control or anything... \n\nBut, um, I guess that is sort of why I decided to come in. It's just that sometimes, when I'm eating alone at night, it feels a little bit like... like maybe something just kind of takes over for a minute? And I find it a bit hard to stop, even when I'm sitting there thinking to myself, \"Okay, I should probably put this away now.\" \n\nIt's not like I'm completely losing my mind or anything! I just get sort of... stuck in the motion of eating, I think? I'm sure a lot of people get carried away with a snack sometimes, right? Like, it's pretty normal to have a hard time putting down a bag of chips when you're tired? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it really is. I hope that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to keep your late-night eating private, so others don't know about it?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do usually eat when I'm alone, yeah. I don't want you to think I'm being sneaky or anything... I hope that answers your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating without thinking about the consequences during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. That's not too unusual, is it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about how it usually goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food during these episodes that's more than what most people would consider a normal amount to eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it might be a little more than a normal dinner, maybe? I might, you know, have a whole frozen pizza. And then... well, I might grab the cereal box and just eat out of that for a bit. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything dramatic like that. I still go to work and do my job, you know? But... I guess it does bother me a little bit sometimes. 

I mean, there was this one time recently... I was doing a bilingual outreach presentation for the shelter, which is usually fine, I like doing them. But I just kept getting so distracted, thinking about getting home and... well, having something to eat. I ended up stumbling through some parts of the presentation because my mind was just somewhere else. I was so worried my coworkers were going to notice I was off my game. 

So, I guess it does get in the way a tiny bit, just with my focus sometimes? But I'm usually really good at my job, I promise. It's just a little hiccup every now and then. That's pretty normal when you're stressed, right? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm terrible at my job...

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. Um... I guess I'm here because... well, I've been having this little issue, and I hope it's okay that I'm bringing it up. \n\nIt's just that, sometimes, when I'm alone at night... I start eating, and it's like... I don't know, it almost feels like something else just takes over? I'll literally be telling myself in my head to just put the food away and go to bed, but I just can't seem to stop. I know it sounds a bit silly, and I'm sure it's probably not the worst thing you've heard today, but... is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when this happens at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um, I guess that's exactly it. Like I was saying, it really does feel like I lose control. It's not like I'm completely out of my mind or anything—I know what I'm doing—but... I just can't seem to stop myself once I start. I'll be standing there in the kitchen, literally thinking to myself, 'Okay, you need to put this away now, just go to bed,' but my hands just keep going. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit that out loud, honestly, because I know I should have more willpower. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Does this loss of control over eating happen at other times of the day as well, or is it mainly at night?\n\nPatient: Um... mostly it's just at night. I mean, during the day, I'm at work—I work with animals, so I'm pretty busy and distracted. Sometimes if I have a really stressful afternoon, I might, you know, have a little extra snack when I get home, but it's not... it doesn't feel like that same out-of-control feeling. \n\nIt's really just when it gets late and quiet, and I'm by myself. I try to be so good during the day, I really do. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd use the word 'ashamed,' really. That sounds a bit... intense, I guess? \n\nI mean, I definitely feel a little silly about it. Maybe just a bit disappointed in my lack of willpower, like I mentioned. But I try not to dwell on it too much, you know? I just kind of wake up the next morning and tell myself that I'll do better that day, and focus on the animals and my work. \n\nI'm not really sure if that's exactly what you meant by guilty? I really hope I'm answering these right for you. I just don't want to make it sound like some huge, dramatic thing when it's probably just me needing to be a bit more disciplined.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, my weight stays pretty much the same, honestly. I try to keep pretty active with the animals and all, so I guess it balances out? \n\nI might fluctuate a pound or two here and there, just like anyone else, but nothing... nothing significant or anything that anyone would notice. I try to take good care of myself, overall. Is that... is that normal? I hope that's okay to say. I just haven't seen any big changes on the scale or anything.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that during these late-night eating episodes, you're consuming a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it depends on what you mean by a large amount? I mean, I don't think it's anything too crazy, but... well, sometimes if I've had a really long day, especially after a late shift at the shelter, I might eat a bit more than usual. \n\nLike, I might make a frozen pizza, and then... maybe have some cereal, just straight out of the box because I don't want to make more dishes, you know? And maybe whatever leftovers are in the fridge, or some snacks from the cabinet. It all just kind of happens in, I don't know, an hour or two while I'm winding down. \n\nI mean, it is definitely more than I would ever eat if someone else was there with me. But I'm just so hungry after running around with the dogs all day. I hope that doesn't sound too terrible? I'm just trying to be completely honest with you so you have what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say I'm really stressed or upset, exactly. I mean, sure, the shelter can be a bit chaotic sometimes, but I love the animals, so it's a good kind of busy, you know? \n\nI think it's more just... well, it's just me unwinding after a long day. It's quiet, and I finally have a moment to myself, so I just sort of relax with some food. It's not like I'm crying or having a panic attack or anything dramatic like that. I'm usually just feeling a bit tired and, I don't know, maybe a little bored? \n\nI really try to keep a positive attitude about things. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... I don't think so? Not really guilty, no. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes the next morning I might think, 'Oh, I probably didn't need to eat quite all of that,' mostly just because I feel a little full or sluggish when I'm getting ready for work. But I try not to be too hard on myself about it. It's just food, you know? I'm not, like, beating myself up or sitting there feeling terrible about myself. I try to give myself some grace, especially since I'm on my feet all day. \n\nIs... is it bad if I don't feel guilty? I hope it's okay that I'm saying that. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating more quickly than usual during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think I eat any faster than normal? I mean, I'm usually just sitting on the couch, maybe watching a show or something while I eat. \n\nIt's not like I'm racing against a clock or shoving food in my mouth as fast as I can. I just kind of eat at a normal pace, I guess. Sometimes I might eat a little quicker just because I'm so tired and I want to get to bed, but it's nothing extreme. \n\nI try to just relax and enjoy it, you know? Does that sound normal to you? I hope I'm explaining it right. I just don't want you to think I'm doing anything too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... I mean, maybe a little bit? But I really don't think it's, like, a crazy amount or anything. \n\nI mean, when I get home after a late shift at the shelter, I'm just so hungry, you know? Being on my feet all day with the dogs and cleaning... it takes a lot out of you. So, yeah, sometimes I might have, like, a frozen pizza. And maybe some cereal if I'm still feeling a bit hungry after that. Or just whatever leftovers or snacks are around. It probably happens over, like, an hour or two while I'm winding down. \n\nI guess if you put it all together, it might sound like a lot to some people, and I probably wouldn't eat quite that much if I had company over... but I'm just trying to refuel after a long day. That makes sense, right? Like, it's normal to be really hungry after working hard? I just want to make sure you understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating is usually triggered by a specific emotional state, such as feeling sad, anxious, or bored?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't really think it's an emotional thing? I mean, I wouldn't say I feel sad or anxious or anything like that. Honestly, I really like my job, and working with the animals keeps me pretty upbeat. \n\nI think it's really just that I'm physically wiped out. By the time I get home, my body is just telling me it needs energy after running around the kennels all day. So I don't think I'm eating because of my feelings or because I'm bored. It's just physical hunger, I think. \n\nThat's pretty standard, right? Like, eating just because you're tired and need to recharge, not because you're upset? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking... I hope that's helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm totally out of control or anything... \n\nBut, um, I guess that is sort of why I decided to come in. It's just that sometimes, when I'm eating alone at night, it feels a little bit like... like maybe something just kind of takes over for a minute? And I find it a bit hard to stop, even when I'm sitting there thinking to myself, \"Okay, I should probably put this away now.\" \n\nIt's not like I'm completely losing my mind or anything! I just get sort of... stuck in the motion of eating, I think? I'm sure a lot of people get carried away with a snack sometimes, right? Like, it's pretty normal to have a hard time putting down a bag of chips when you're tired? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it really is. I hope that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to keep your late-night eating private, so others don't know about it?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do usually eat when I'm alone, yeah. But it's not like I'm hiding it or anything! It's just that I live by myself, and I work late, so there's usually no one else around anyway. \n\nI mean, if someone were there, I probably wouldn't eat quite the same way, just because I'd be talking to them or whatever. But it's not some big secret. I just prefer to unwind by myself after a long day at the shelter. That's pretty normal, right? Just wanting some quiet time alone to eat and relax? I don't want you to think I'm being sneaky or anything... I hope that answers your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating without thinking about the consequences during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm not thinking about the consequences at all. \n\nI know it's not the healthiest habit to eat so much right before bed, and I do think about that. It's just that, like I said, I'm so tired, and it just kind of happens. But I'm definitely aware of it, you know? It's not like I just completely check out or forget that eating a bunch of food late at night might make me feel sluggish the next day. \n\nI try to be pretty mindful of things, usually. I just don't want you to think I'm completely careless or oblivious about my health or anything. I'm sure everyone has moments where they know they shouldn't have that extra snack but do it anyway, right? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't think I eat super fast or anything like that. I mean, maybe a little quicker than if I was sitting down for a nice dinner at a restaurant, but... I don't think it's anything crazy? \n\nI just kind of eat at a normal pace, I guess. I mean, I'm usually just watching TV or something while I eat, so I'm not, like, shoveling it in. It's more just... steady, I suppose? I definitely don't want it to sound like I'm just inhaling my food without even chewing it or anything gross like that. \n\nI'm sure everyone eats a little faster when they're really hungry after a long shift, right? That's not too unusual, is it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about how it usually goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food during these episodes that's more than what most people would consider a normal amount to eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it might be a little more than a normal dinner, maybe? Sometimes I'll make a frozen pizza, and... well, I might eat the whole thing. And then maybe have some cereal, just, you know, out of the box while I'm watching TV. And if there are leftovers or snacks in the cabinet, I might have some of those too, over the course of an hour or two. \n\nI know it sounds like a lot when I list it out like that, but I'm just so famished after work! I definitely wouldn't eat that much if I was out with friends or something. I just don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or anything like that. It's just a hungry guy unwinding, right? I hope that makes sense and doesn't sound too bad?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the amount of food you eat during these late-night episodes is excessively large?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess \"excessively large\" sounds a bit... harsh? I hope I'm explaining it right. I just don't want you to think I'm doing anything too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... I mean, maybe a little bit? But I really don't think it's, like, a crazy amount or anything. \n\nI mean, when I get home after a late shift at the shelter, I'm just so hungry, you know? Being on my feet all day with the dogs and cleaning... it takes a lot out of you. So, yeah, sometimes I might have, like, a frozen pizza. And maybe some cereal if I'm still feeling a bit hungry after that. Or just whatever leftovers or snacks are around. It probably happens over, like, an hour or two while I'm winding down. \n\nI guess if you put it all together, it might sound like a lot to some people, and I probably wouldn't eat quite that much if I had company over... but I'm just trying to refuel after a long day. That makes sense, right? Like, it's normal to be really hungry after working hard? I just want to make sure you understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating is usually triggered by a specific emotional state, such as feeling sad, anxious, or bored?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't really think it's an emotional thing? I mean, I wouldn't say I feel sad or anxious or anything like that. Honestly, I really like my job, and working with the animals keeps me pretty upbeat. \n\nI think it's really just that I'm physically wiped out. By the time I get home, my body is just telling me it needs energy after running around the kennels all day. So I don't think I'm eating because of my feelings or because I'm bored. It's just physical hunger, I think. \n\nThat's pretty standard, right? Like, eating just because you're tired and need to recharge, not because you're upset? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking... I hope that's helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... maybe a little bit? It's just that I live by myself, and I work late, so there's usually no one else around anyway. \n\nI mean, if someone were there, I probably wouldn't eat quite the same way, just because I'd be talking to them or whatever. It's just that, like I said, I'm so tired, and it just kind of happens. But I'm definitely aware of it, you know? I just don't want you to think I'm completely careless or oblivious about my health or anything. It's quiet, and I finally have a moment to myself, so I just sort of relax with some food. It's not like I'm crying or having a panic attack or anything dramatic like that. I'm usually just feeling a bit tired and, I don't know, maybe a little bored? \n\nI really try to keep a positive attitude about things. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... I don't think so? Not really guilty, no. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes the next morning I might think, 'Oh, I probably didn't need to eat quite all of that,' mostly just because I feel a little full or sluggish when I'm getting ready for work. But I try not to be too hard on myself about it. It's just food, you know? I'm not, like, beating myself up or sitting there feeling terrible about myself. I try to give myself some grace, especially since I'm on my feet all day. \n\nIs... is it bad if I don't feel guilty? I hope it's okay that I'm saying that. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating more quickly than usual during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think I eat any faster than normal? I mean, I'm usually just sitting on the couch, maybe watching a show or something while I eat. \n\nIt's not like I'm racing against a clock or shoving food in my mouth as fast as I can. I just kind of eat at a normal pace, I guess. Sometimes I might eat a little quicker just because I'm so tired and I want to get to bed, but it's nothing extreme. \n\nI try to just relax and enjoy it, you know? Does that sound normal to you? I hope I'm explaining it right. I just don't want you to think I'm doing anything too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... I mean, maybe a little bit? But I really don't think it's, like, a crazy amount or anything. \n\nI mean, when I get home after a late shift at the shelter, I'm just so hungry, you know? Being on my feet all day with the dogs and cleaning... it takes a lot out of you. So, yeah, sometimes I might have, like, a frozen pizza. And maybe some cereal if I'm still feeling a bit hungry after that. Or just whatever leftovers or snacks are around. It probably happens over, like, an hour or two while I'm winding down. \n\nI guess if you put it all together, it might sound like a lot to some people, and I probably wouldn't eat quite that much if I had company over... but I'm just trying to refuel after a long day. That makes sense, right? Like, it's normal to be really hungry after working hard? I just want to make sure you understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating is usually triggered by a specific emotional state, such as feeling sad, anxious, or bored?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't really think it's an emotional thing? I mean, I wouldn't say I feel sad or anxious or anything like that. Honestly, I really like my job, and working with the animals keeps me pretty upbeat. \n\nI think it's really just that I'm physically wiped out. By the time I get home, my body is just telling me it needs energy after running around the kennels all day. So I don't think I'm eating because of my feelings or because I'm bored. It's just physical hunger, I think. \n\nThat's pretty standard, right? Like, eating just because you're tired and need to recharge, not because you're upset? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking... I hope that's helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really don't want to sound like I'm totally out of control or anything... \n\nBut, um, I guess that is sort of why I decided to come in. It's just that sometimes, when I'm eating alone at night, it feels a little bit like... like maybe something just kind of takes over for a minute? And I find it a bit hard to stop, even when I'm sitting there thinking to myself, \"Okay, I should probably put this away now.\" \n\nIt's not like I'm completely losing my mind or anything! I just get sort of... stuck in the motion of eating, I think? I'm sure a lot of people get carried away with a snack sometimes, right? Like, it's pretty normal to have a hard time putting down a bag of chips when you're tired? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it really is. I hope that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to keep your late-night eating private, so others don't know about it?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do usually eat when I'm alone, yeah. But it's not like I'm hiding it or anything! It's just that I live by myself, and I work late, so there's usually no one else around anyway. \n\nI mean, if someone were there, I probably wouldn't eat quite the same way, just because I'd be talking to them or whatever. But it's not some big secret. I just prefer to unwind by myself after a long day at the shelter. That's pretty normal, right? Just wanting some quiet time alone to eat and relax? I don't want you to think I'm being sneaky or anything... I hope that answers your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating without thinking about the consequences during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm not thinking about the consequences at all. \n\nI know it's not the healthiest habit to eat so much right before bed, and I do think about that. It's just that, like I said, I'm so tired, and it just kind of happens. But I'm definitely aware of it, you know? It's not like I just completely check out or forget that eating a bunch of food late at night might make me feel sluggish the next day. \n\nI try to be pretty mindful of things, usually. I just don't want you to think I'm completely careless or oblivious about my health or anything. I'm sure everyone has moments where they know they shouldn't have that extra snack but do it anyway, right? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't think I eat super fast or anything like that. I mean, maybe a little quicker than if I was sitting down for a nice dinner at a restaurant, but... I don't think it's anything crazy? \n\nI just kind of eat at a normal pace, I guess. I mean, I'm usually just watching TV or something while I eat, so I'm not, like, shoveling it in. It's more just... steady, I suppose? I definitely don't want it to sound like I'm just inhaling my food without even chewing it or anything gross like that. \n\nI'm sure everyone eats a little faster when they're really hungry after a long shift, right? That's not too unusual, is it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about how it usually goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating an amount of food during these episodes that's more than what most people would consider a normal amount to eat in one sitting?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it might be a little more than a normal dinner, maybe? I don't know if I'd say it's, like, a huge amount or anything crazy. \n\nI mean, on nights when I've had a really late shift at the shelter, I get so hungry. Sometimes I'll make a frozen pizza, and... well, I might eat the whole thing. And then maybe have some cereal, just, you know, out of the box while I'm watching TV. And if there are leftovers or snacks in the cabinet, I might have some of those too, over the course of an hour or two. \n\nI know it sounds like a lot when I list it out like that, but I'm just so famished after work! I definitely wouldn't eat that much if I was out with friends or something. I just don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or anything like that. It's just a hungry guy unwinding, right? I hope that makes sense and doesn't sound too bad?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the amount of food you eat during these late-night episodes is excessively large?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess \"excessively large\" sounds a bit... harsh? But, um, I mean, I know we just talked about this, and I don't want to seem like I'm hiding anything from you. \n\nLike I said, it's usually after those really late shifts at the shelter. I'd be so embarrassed to eat like that around my friends. But I just get so hungry, and I don't want you to think it's some terrible, out-of-control thing. It's just a bad habit when I'm tired, you know? I really hope you don't think too badly of me for it... does that kind of answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these late-night eating episodes are causing you significant distress or interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything dramatic like that. I still go to work and do my job, you know? But... I guess it does bother me a little bit sometimes. \n\nI mean, there was this one time recently... I was doing a bilingual outreach presentation for the shelter, which is usually fine, I like doing them. But I just kept getting so distracted, thinking about getting home and... well, having something to eat.
